Buildrick Deep UI and Modal Audit — 7 September 2026Buildrick · Product design review · 07 September 2026
Deep UI audit:
editor, modals and popup states.
The linked Editor v1 page has extensive state coverage, but assembled screens still disagree with their panels and newer corrections. The most immediate issues are contradictory selection, obscured controls, clipped recovery guidance and obsolete prototype destinations.
Source: Editor v1 ↗ · Reference: Editor v2 — Proposal ↗29Editor v1 sections scanned
614Frames inventoried, including reference and archived frames
27Visual samples across both passes
4828 initial findings + 20 modal observations/checks

First-pass coverage and completion limitThe full Editor v1 page received a structural scan of 42,007 descendants. The inventory records screen/panel/modal/reference frame names, dimensions, text excerpts, component-instance counts, prototype links and selected layout checks. Twenty-five V1 frames and the V2 final editor were visually inspected. Figma then returned its tool-call limit, preventing the remaining per-state visual checks. This was the first full-page structural pass with a sampled visual audit. The added modal pass below records refreshed checks and the continuing visual coverage limit. No section claims that every screen has passed visual QA.
Figma designs and their annotations cannot verify current runtime behavior. No repository, live application, data persistence, real publish job or keyboard behavior was tested. Existing code-level audit claims are identified below as a validation queue. No design changes were made.

Deep modal auditUI correction rules95 modal-related entriesInitial 28 findingsModule coverageRuntime validation queueSearchable frame inventoryFix order and acceptanceWhat is already presentThere are distinct empty, loading, failure, confirmation and recovery specimens across Media, CMS, History, Review, Publish and Settings. Inspector profiles, page deletion impact, stock-provider errors, AI recovery and other newer corrections are also drawn. The current problem is not a general absence of states; it is whether those states are coherent, readable, connected and incorporated into the assembled experience.
Prototype check: all 525 distinct referenced destination IDs inspected resolve to nodes. Some still resolve to explicitly retired designs. A valid node ID is not proof of a correct user journey. The audit found 3,569 nodes with reactions and 912 nodes named hotspot/…; neither count is a count of unique user flows.

Deep UI pass: modals, popups and state fidelity
Main finding: Several newly named states were copied without changing their content. A layer called single-file, all-failed, refused or inbound-links does not yet communicate that state in the interface. These are concrete design defects before any backend behavior is considered.
This pass adds 20 observations and checks to the initial 28. It reviewed 98 candidate inventory entries, retained 95 modal/popup/state/reference entries after excluding three unrelated matches, refreshed six relevant nodes/boards, and obtained one additional modal screenshot. The remaining entries use the earlier recorded Figma structure and text. Figma returned its call limit again. Every modal has not received a fresh visual inspection.

P1M01Fresh screenshot + current node textSingle-file delete is still the bulk-delete design
Observed. The frame named Media · delete-confirm · single file says Delete 34 files, warns about 5 used files and requires DELETE. Its current text matches the bulk-delete specimen.
Fix. Create a real single-asset state with the filename, one asset's usage impact and an action such as Delete hero-kitchen.jpg. Keep bulk typed-confirmation as a separate variant.
Accept when. Frame name, asset count, affected usage and primary action all describe the same selection.

Fresh screenshot of frame 2850:22371. The frame name says single file; the visible dialog says Delete 34 files.
P1M02Fresh node text + hierarchyAll-failed rollback state contains success and partial success
Observed. The all-failed (rollback) frame contains Replaced 3 uses ✓ and 2 replaced, 1 failed. Current children remain result-clean and result-partial; no all-failed outcome is drawn.
Fix. Draw a dedicated rollback result: No replacements applied; original images restored, if that is the actual operation contract. Show failed targets and Retry failed. Use separate partial and complete success variants.
Accept when. All-failed, partial and success states have distinct, internally consistent counts, status and recovery actions.

P1M03Fresh node text + hierarchyAdd Child refused state has no refusal message
Observed. The refused frame's complete current text is only Add Child Element, search and BASIC/MEDIA/LAYOUT/FORMS. It matches the default picker text.
Fix. Add a visible reason such as This element cannot contain children, identify the selected target and provide Select another parent / Choose a compatible element. Keep the user's search and selection.
Accept when. A reviewer can distinguish refusal from default without reading the Figma layer name.
2850:21560 ↗ · 1706:8501 ↗
P1M04Retained full text comparisonPage-deletion impact dialog is an unsaved-SEO copy
Observed. The frame named delete-page · confirm · inbound links contains exactly the same recorded text as the unsaved-warning dialog: Discard unsaved SEO changes? No page deletion or inbound links are described.
Fix. Use Delete [page name]?; state affected internal links and navigation references; provide Review affected links plus Cancel / Delete page. Define home-page and dynamic-template restrictions as separate states.
Accept when. The deletion dialog names the page, explains dependency impact and uses Delete page as its destructive action.
2898:22178 ↗ · 1171:4820 ↗
P1M05Fresh exact geometryCreate Component Cancel overlaps the primary button
Observed. Cancel text occupies x=520–563, y=698–714. Create component occupies x=548–700, y=690–724. Their rectangles overlap by 15×16 pixels.
Fix. Place both actions in one horizontal auto-layout footer with an explicit gap. Make Cancel a complete button instance and give the primary button content-based width.
Accept when. Action hit areas and label bounds do not intersect; labels retain their padding after text changes.

P1M06Fresh screenshot + geometryDestructive warning is clipped inside its alert
Observed. The delete warning is a 408×25 region containing a long warning at 11px. The new screenshot cuts off the final word elements at the right edge.
Fix. Use a wrapping alert body with an independent icon column and content-based height. Keep the entire dependency warning above the confirmation input.
Accept when. The full warning fits at 440px modal width and with longer filenames/localized text.

P1M07Fresh complete node textBrand change review displays four changes but says three
Observed. Review 3 staged changes and Apply 3 changes are shown alongside four rows: color/accent, type/heading, spacing/lg and shadow/raised removed.
Fix. Generate the title and CTA count from the same reviewed change set, including deletions. If a change is unsupported, separate it and explain whether it will be applied.
Accept when. Visible applied rows and both counts agree; unsupported rows never look included in an executable action.
1172:4840 ↗
P1M08Retained complete textSingle-image picker shows multiple selection
Observed. Choose an image / for Hero · Image shares the same specimen with 2 selected and Use Selected.
Fix. Define selection mode by destination. A single-image property gets a radio-style single choice and Use image; multi-asset insertion gets a separate plural title and count-aware action.
Accept when. A single-image target cannot be represented as accepting two assets; title, count and CTA agree.
1164:4713 ↗
P1M09Retained complete textCMS publish error contradicts the record's own state
Observed. The publish-error record shows Margherita €9.00 published while the message says Can't publish: Price is required.
Fix. Draw the actual invalid field empty, preserve the draft/unpublished status and highlight the affected price input with an Edit record action. Keep currency consistent with the collection example.
Accept when. The displayed value, validation message and publish status describe one coherent failed transaction.
1706:8451 ↗
P2M10Retained complete textNo-published-records state sends users to create more records
Observed. The dialog says Menu items — 12 records and No records published yet, but its only task CTA is + Add record.
Fix. Offer Review drafts / Select records to publish for the 12 existing records. Keep Add record secondary; show the missing-template dependency separately.
Accept when. A user can publish an existing valid record from the recovery path without creating a thirteenth record.
1706:8437 ↗
P2M11Retained complete textNo-collections dialog explains a detour without providing it
Observed. The complete 720×80 dialog says to create a collection from an element's CMS binding, with only a close symbol.
Fix. Provide Create collection or Open Content with an explicit destination. Avoid sending users through an unrelated element-selection workflow when a direct Content route exists.
Accept when. The empty state provides a direct, labeled path to its prerequisite.
1706:8433 ↗ · 148:2 ↗
P1M12Retained complete textAI success has no way to use the result
Observed. The AI success modal presents PricingCard JSON with Retry and Discard. There is no Add component, Apply or usable preview action in the recorded text.
Fix. Replace raw schema with a rendered preview and a clear Apply/Add component action when supported. Otherwise label the result as unavailable/unsupported and explain what the user can do.
Accept when. A successful generation can be previewed and applied, or is explicitly identified as non-applicable rather than success.
1706:8483 ↗
P2M13Retained complete textAI not-configured state suggests Retry
Observed. The error dialog says AI service not configured but offers Retry / Cancel.
Fix. Use Configure AI or an appropriate Contact workspace admin path for a configuration problem. Reserve Retry for transient provider failures; preserve the prompt.
Accept when. The recovery action addresses the actual failure category instead of repeating an unchanged request.
1706:8492 ↗
P2M14Retained measured dimensionsCMS dialog width changes between default and error/empty variants
Observed. The records table is 640px wide; no-collections, none-published, empty and publish-error variants are 720px wide. The default frame explicitly says its width stays 640.
Fix. Use one CMS records dialog shell across states. Let the body change while header, width and footer anchors remain stable.
Accept when. Loading, populated, empty and failed states use the same width and do not jump sideways.

P2M15Retained text; sample-content inconsistencyCollection wizard uses a mismatched example configuration
Observed. The collection is Menu items, with Title/Price/Photo/Description fields, while its generation line uses /blog/{slug}, blog/_template and an SEO title ending Blog.
Fix. Use a consistent restaurant-menu example and expose editable template/URL fields. Make the required Slug field visible or explain which field supplies it.
Accept when. Collection fields, generated URLs, template and SEO examples refer to the same content type.

P2M16Retained complete textNo-records binding popover offers the wrong prerequisite
Observed. The binding popover says No records yet. Open Content › this collection to add the first one, but the visible CTA is + Create Collection.
Fix. Use Add record to [collection] or Open collection. Only offer Create collection when no collection exists.
Accept when. The primary action resolves the missing record in the selected collection.

P2M17Retained complete text; visible-label checkSave-as-template field has no visible label
Observed. The save-as-template specimen contains its title, Autumn menu layout, explanatory copy and Cancel / Save template, but no visible Template name field label.
Fix. Add a persistent Template name label, required marker if appropriate, and an inline duplicate/invalid-name error state. Keep the user's entered name on error.
Accept when. The field remains identifiable after its value is cleared, and validation appears beside the field.

P2M18Retained complete textCanvas AI error popover ends at a failure sentence
Observed. The 248×40 error specimen only says The AI service didn't respond.
Fix. Retain the prompt and add Retry / Keep editing or an equivalent return route. Use a stable popover body/footer so the controls do not disappear after a request.
Accept when. The user can recover or return to editing from the error state without losing their prompt.

P2M19Fresh typography + geometryNew modal specimens drift from the existing modal typography and control scale
Observed. The reference kit uses 16/24 titles and 13/20 action text. The single-delete dialog uses a 14px title, 11px action text and 27px footer controls; Brand change review also has 11px action labels and a 27px footer.
Fix. Reuse the established modal title/body/button styles and shared footer component. Keep compact technical metadata distinct from important action labels.
Accept when. Equivalent modal roles reuse the same title, body and button styles rather than independent local values.

ReviewM20Measured geometry; viewport behavior requires visual/prototype verificationLong Create Component modal needs a constrained-height state
Observed. The dialog is 720×740 before external margins. At a 720px-tall window the frame already exceeds the viewport. The current inspected hierarchy has absolute-positioned form groups and its footer at y=676–724.
Fix. Add a viewport-constrained variant with fixed header/footer and a scrolling form body. Test 1280×720 and increased text size, preserving visible Cancel/Create actions.
Accept when. All form fields remain reachable and the primary action is visible at the agreed minimum editor window height.

Concrete UI correction specification
These are proposed acceptance rules for the existing Buildrick design. Typography values below reuse the inspected modal reference kit where available. Dimensions proposed for responsive behavior must be agreed and validated; they are not claims about the existing implementation.
 |  | UI area
 | Correction rule
 | Check

 | Modal anatomy
 | One shell: header with title and Close, content body, inline feedback region, footer. Place engineering notes outside the dialog.
 | A frame export contains only user-facing content.

 | Widths
 | Use 440px for concise confirmation, 640px for the existing records family, and 720px for complex component work. Treat these as role-based sizes, not interchangeable variants of the same state. Full-screen scenario frames and multi-state boards are not modal widths.
 | A dialog's width remains stable across its loading, empty and error variants.

 | Height / scroll
 | Proposed maximum height: viewport height minus 48px. Keep the header and footer fixed; scroll only the form body. Center short confirmation dialogs.
 | At 1280×720, Create Component remains fully operable with its actions visible.

 | Typography
 | Use the reference kit's title 16px / 24px, body and action text 13px / 20px, label 12px / 18px, and input text 14px / 20px. Bind styles instead of assigning local AUTO line heights.
 | Equivalent content uses the same text style in every modal family.

 | Spacing
 | Use consistent token-based padding, with 20–24px proposed for dialog body and footer; 8px between label and field; 16px between field groups; 8–12px between actions. Do not place footer labels with absolute coordinates.
 | Long labels and validation messages expand without collisions.

 | Buttons
 | One clear primary action; place secondary action consistently before it. Use shared button instances, content-based widths and an explicit gap. Name destructive actions and their subject: Delete page, Unpublish site, Discard 12 changes.
 | Labels fit, hit areas do not overlap, and no unrelated helper hotspot covers a button.

 | Forms
 | Keep persistent visible labels; mark optional/required consistently; retain entered values on validation or network errors. Put field errors beside the affected field and request-level errors above the footer.
 | Clearing an input never removes the only explanation of what the field is for.

 | Choice controls
 | Single target means one selected item; bulk actions must show count and scope. Checkbox, radio and row selection must have clearly different semantics.
 | Choose an image for Hero cannot show 2 selected.

 | Destructive confirmation
 | Name the object and dependency impact; distinguish reversible deletion, permanent deletion, draft restoration and live rollback. Keep typed confirmation restricted to its agreed high-impact use cases.
 | Single/bulk and draft/live variants cannot be confused.

 | Dismiss / dirty state
 | Define Close, Escape, backdrop and Cancel as one consistent contract. A dirty form must give Keep editing / Discard changes; a running job must explain whether closing stops it or lets it continue.
 | Prototype or runtime testing confirms each dismissal path. Static screenshots alone cannot prove it.

 | Focus / keyboard
 | Specify initial focus, visible focus state, focus containment, return-to-trigger behavior and shortcut suspension while a modal is active. Prefer the safe action as initial focus in irreversible confirmations.
 | Verify by keyboard in prototype/runtime; not certified by this audit.

 | Color / readability
 | Retain Buildrick's established accent, neutral and status tokens. Separate disabled state from warning/error and avoid color-only status. Measure text/background pairs after checking actual fills and opacity.
 | Contrast and focus checks remain pending where the needed visual/property evidence was unavailable.

State matrix to complete for each applicable modal
 | State
 | What must be drawn

 | Default
 | Realistic task title, target, fields and clear primary action.

 | Focused / edited
 | Focus outline, preserved values, dirty status and close behavior.

 | Validation error
 | Actual invalid value, inline field error and correction route.

 | Submitting
 | Busy action, duplicate-submit protection and clear cancellation contract, only if an asynchronous operation produces this state.

 | Network / provider error
 | Preserved work plus Retry. A not-configured error needs setup or administrator help instead.

 | Success
 | Correct result counts and a meaningful next action; do not substitute a success-looking frame for an unsupported result.

 | Partial / all failed
 | Separate successful and failed counts, failed targets, rollback status where applicable and recovery scoped to failures.

 | No data / no results
 | Distinguish no collection, no records, no published records and no search matches; give the action that resolves that exact condition.

 | Permission / unavailable
 | Reason plus a valid access/setup route; retain context rather than silently hiding the task.

 | Long content / small window
 | Wrapping titles, long names, expanded errors and body scrolling with visible footer actions.

Correction order: resolve M01–M09 first; then fix recovery actions and state-family consistency; then apply the shared modal shell and complete keyboard, long-content and viewport verification. The initial editor-wide findings below remain relevant. This pass audited the design; it did not modify any Figma node.

Initial editor-wide findings
P1: fix before design sign-off because the task, state or destination is misleading or obstructed. P2: correct for consistency, clarity or handoff quality. Evidence labels distinguish a screenshot observation from a structural candidate.
P1F01 · Shell / Layers / InspectorVisualSelection disagrees across the editor
Evidence. The assembled editor highlights “heading-that-is-long” in Layers, while the canvas selection label and Inspector identify “Section”. The selected-element variant retains “Section” while outlining the heading area.
Impact. Users cannot confidently predict which element an Inspector edit will change.
Correction. Bind the layer highlight, canvas outline, breadcrumb and Inspector header to one selected element. Draw a consistent heading-selected and section-selected pair.
Accept when. All four locations identify the same element in each selection state.

Recorded product risks to validate next
These are claims already present in the Figma file. They were not independently reproduced in this audit. They are not included as confirmed application defects or added to the 28 findings.
 |  | Area
 | Recorded claim
 | Required runtime check

 | CMS persistence and conditions ↗
 | The file records reload loss for sources and conditions whose output is not honored.
 | Create a source, bind an element, reload, reopen on a second device and compare preview/published output.

 | Page state and routing ↗
 | The V2 board records draft/visibility and slug-collision risks.
 | Edit an unrelated setting on a draft page; confirm status remains draft. Try duplicate and renamed slugs and inspect internal links.

 | Publishing after lost contact ↗
 | The file records polling failures that leave an in-progress publish stranded.
 | Disconnect during a publish, reconnect, recover job status, retry safely and confirm the final deployment URL.

 | Preview parity ↗
 | The file documents restrictions around scripts, forms, frames and the active-page preview.
 | Test navigation, forms, scripts, CMS pages and interactions against a deployed staging site.

 | Layers locking and visibility ↗
 | The file records divergent lock sources and editor-only dim behavior.
 | Lock/dim elements, reload elsewhere and verify selection and published visibility match their labels.

 | AI apply, scope and undo ↗
 | The file records exceptions to a universal propose/diff/apply/undo contract.
 | Test every AI entry point, including alt text and SEO title, for consent, scope, cancel, retry and one-step undo.

 | History and backups ↗
 | Backup specimens are explicitly labeled not implemented.
 | Verify actual saved versions, restoration boundaries, reload retention and whether a backup service exists.

 | Commerce and integrations ↗
 | The integrations specimen is labeled not implemented while displaying connected services; commerce setup alone does not prove checkout.
 | Configure a payment provider, preview a product, complete a test checkout and inspect order/customer outcomes and failure states.

 | Rail · selected vs open (two signals) — UX-F-02: selected and open are two signals, not one. aria-selected and the accent bar are both bound to “active AND drawer open” (LeftSidebar.tsx:137-160), so with the drawer shut the rail reports a tablist with nothing chosen. Fix order and acceptance
Correct user-facing contradictions and unreadable content. Fix selection/fill identity, Pages and Media overlap, notification rows, AI/History recovery guidance and Review semantics.
Assemble the accepted shell and shared components. Apply the agreed navigation, headers, buttons, panel widths, full-page mode and motion model to actual state families.
Rewire the active prototype. Replace obsolete destinations and keep archives outside active journeys. Check real control targets, not only helper hotspots.
Validate six complete tasks. Build and publish a page; create CMS content and a dynamic page; upload/replace an asset; request and resolve review; restore a version; recover from a Settings or publishing failure.
Finish visual QA for every remaining frame. Inspect normal, hover/focus, long-content, loading, empty, failure, permission and recovery variants at their intended dimensions. Treat 614 as an artifact inventory, not a unique-screen total.
Design sign-off requires matching selection context, readable actions and recovery guidance, coherent navigation, current prototype destinations and per-frame visual verification. Product sign-off additionally requires reproducing the runtime checks above.
